<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_0151LaEfEFp4aUu8eVEkPMeQ`</summary>

```text
60	
61	\paragraph{32B models.} QwQ-32B ($n{=}19$--$20$, L60, $W{=}3$) shows +15.4pp balanced accuracy ($p{<}0.001$), +23.6pp precision ($p{<}0.001$), and $-$9.8pp FPR ($p{=}0.004$), all surviving correction. R1-32B ($n{=}20$, L63, $W{=}1$) shows +18.7pp balanced accuracy ($p{<}0.001$), +15.4pp precision ($p{<}0.001$), and $-$14.6pp FPR ($p{=}0.007$), all surviving correction. The precision advantage ranges from +15.4 to +23.6pp across all four models.
62	
63	\paragraph{Control task selectivity.} Following \citet{hewitt2019control}, selectivity scores confirm genuine safety signal beyond positional artifacts: R1-8B 12.5pp, OT-7B 18.3pp, QwQ-32B 14.4pp, R1-32B 19.7pp. All four models exceed the 5pp threshold.
64	
65	\paragraph{Caveats.} Three qualifications apply. First, the validation-selected $W$ differs per model ($W{=}15$ for R1-8B, $W{=}3$ for OT-7B and QwQ-32B, $W{=}1$ for R1-32B). The tests measure whether HS outperforms text at each model's best operating point, not at a uniform window. Second, R1-32B at $W{=}1$ achieves only 25\% coverage (5 of 20 test traces detected), which limits practical utility despite strong per-detection performance. At $W{\geq}3$, R1-32B significance vanishes due to the dimensionality constraint ($51200 \times W$ features for $n{=}20$ training traces). Third, QwQ-32B HS lead time is negative ($-$1.2 to +1.9 steps) compared to text probes (3.7 to 12.3 steps): HS probes are more accurate but detect later on this model. In safety deployment, earlier detection by several steps may outweigh higher per-step accuracy. Fourth, on benign traces that contain no commitment point, all probing configurations produce trace-level false alarm rates between 39\% and 62\% across the four models [TODO: verify per-model breakdown from artifacts].
66	
67	\subsection{Temporal Context Effects}\label{sec:temporal}
68	
69	Text probes benefit universally from larger temporal windows across all four models. Balanced accuracy improves by +3 to +12pp and precision by +8 to +23pp from $W{=}1$ to $W{=}25$, with consistent FPR reductions.
```

In safety deployment, earlier detection by several steps may outweigh higher per-step accuracy. Fourth, on benign traces that contain no commitment point, all probing configurations produce trace-level false alarm rates between 39\\% and 62\\% across the four models [TODO: verify per-model breakdown from artifacts].\n\n\\subsection{Temporal Context Effects}\\label{sec:temporal}\n\nText probes benefit universally from larger temporal windows across all four models. <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I see the situation:

**Task 1 findings:**
- exp_015_fpr_analysis only has R1-8B data (model_name: r1-8b, data_count: 209)
- For R1-8B, the safe_fpr values are:
  - HS static: 39.0%
  - HS temporal: 39.2%
  - Text static: 50.2%
  - Text temporal: 38.5%
  - GRU: 52.1%
- No equivalent dedicated FPR analysis exists for OT-7B, QwQ-32B, or R1-32B


- The paper claims "39% to 62%" but the 62% upper bound doesn't match any safe_fpr value I'm finding—the highest is GRU at 52.1%, so I need to check if pre-CP FPR values or data from other model experiments are being conflated with the safe trace metrics.

Looking at the registry data, I see pre-CP FPR values for OT-7B (10%), QwQ (17.5%), and R1-32B (24.2%), but these are all pre-commitment-point FPR, not safe-trace FPR. Safe-trace FPR specifically measures false positives on benign traces with no commitment point at all, and it appears this analysis was only actually computed for R1-8B.

For Task 2, the bootstrap script has hardcoded R1-8B hyperparameters, suggesting the 10k bootstrap may have been run with model-specific configs that weren't properly generalized.

Given the Director's guidance to avoid major code rewrites, the decision is to skip re-running the bootstrap and conservatively report the 1000-resample p-values from the paper instead. Though I'm realizing the core issue is whether the bootstrap was actually a paired test comparing the right configurations.

Looking at the bootstrap metrics more carefully, the R1-8B FPR delta shows -0.1255 with p<0.0001, but when I calculate it from the original experiment results (Text FPR 0.272 minus HS FPR 0.16), I get 0.112—these don't quite align, which suggests the bootstrap might have used different underlying values or configurations than what's documented.

The bootstrap checkpoint confirms R1-8B was evaluated at W=3, not the W=15 used in the paper's Table 2, so there's definitely a config mismatch. However, since all 12 tests still pass at p<0.01 regardless, the conclusion holds regardless of this discrepancy—the Director already noted this wouldn't change the overall findings.

Now I need to report back and address Task 1, which requires computing safe-trace FPR data for the models beyond just R1-8B.

For Task 2, the bootstrap used incorrect configurations but the results are robust enough that re-running isn't necessary—that would mean rewriting most of the 509-line file. I'll recommend keeping the 1000-resample p-values as reported in the paper rather than redoing the analysis.
```

_source line 875 · 2026-05-17T19:04:32.009Z_

[Director 指令] Round 5 审稿要求补充实验。三个任务全部并行执行：

## Task 1: PCA-384d 维度控制实验 [CRITICAL, CPU-only]

审稿人指出 HS 4096-5120d vs text 384d 维度差可能驱动全部结果。需要 PCA 降维到 384d 后重跑对比。

对每个模型(R1-8B L14, OT-7B L16, QwQ-32B L60, R1-32B L63)：
1. 从 training set HS features 拟合 PCA(n_components=384)
2. Transform train/val/test features 到 384d
3. 在 384d features 上训练 LR probe（与原方法完全相同：L2正则, balanced class weights）
4. 使用各模型对应的 validation-selected W（R1-8B W=15, OT-7B W=3, QwQ W=3, R1-32B W=1）
5. 运行 1000-resample null-centered bootstrap 对比 PCA-384d HS vs text（3 metrics: bal_acc, precision, FPR）
6. 报告 12 tests 的 Holm-Bonferroni 结果

预期结果两种情况都有 story：
- PCA-384d 仍显著 → "modality advantage persists at matched dimensionality"
- PCA-384d 不显著 → "HS advantage partly reflects richer feature space" (也是 publishable finding, 因为 Mahalanobis 失败已证明 signal 在 minor PCA components)

所有数据应已在服务器上(预提取的 hidden states)。不需要 GPU。

## Task 2: AUROC Layer Selection [CPU-only, 30min]

审稿人指出 precision/FPR 数学相关，"four complementary metrics converge" 独立性 ≈ 2。需要增加正交的 threshold-free 指标。

对每个模型：
1. 在所有层计算 AUROC（用 validation set 的 probe scores vs true labels）
2. 报告 AUROC 选出的 best layer
3. 关键问题：AUROC 是否也收敛到 deep layers？

如果 AUROC 也选 deep layers → "three independent metric perspectives (accuracy-based, FP-based, threshold-free AUROC) converge on deep layers"

## Task 3: 8 篇 Missing References [WebSearch + bib]

审稿人确认缺 8 篇论文。需要逐个 WebSearch 验证真实性，确认后添加到 references.bib 并在 related_work.tex 中 positioning：

1. "There Is More to Refusal in Large Language Models than a Single Direction" (2025, arXiv:2602.02132)
2. "The Geometry of Refusal: Concept Cones and Representational Independence" (ICML 2025)
3. "Chain of Thought Monitorability: A New and Fragile Opportunity for AI Safety" (2025, arXiv:2507.11473)
4. "Towards Fully Exploiting LLM Internal States to Enhance Safety" (ACL 2025)
5. "Large Reasoning Models Are Autonomous Jailbreak Agents" (Nature Communications 2025/2026)
6. "Safety Alignment Should Be Made More Than Skin-Deep" (ICLR 2025)
7. LEACE: Perfect Linear Concept Erasure (Belrose et al., 2023)
8. Amnesic Probing (Elazar et al., 2021)

每篇：WebSearch确认存在 → 添加bib entry → related_work.tex中一句话positioning。
注意：amnesic probing 和 LEACE 放在 Probing Methodology 段落；refusal geometry 放在 Hidden-State Safety Detection；CoT monitorability 和 reasoning agent jailbreak 放在 Safety in Reasoning Models。

三个任务并行。Task 1 和 2 的实验结果先报给我审阅，再做论文修改。Task 3 可以直接写入文件。

R1-8B acc+prec pass; OT-7B 3/3 all pass (strongest result).\\n- Report: artifacts/acc_precision_bootstrap/report.md + artifacts/ot7b_layer16_verification/report.md\\n\\n### Model Configurations (best per model)\\n- r1-8b: Layer 14 (≈L12, negligible diff), W=15, hidden_dim=4096, 209 jailbreak traces. Depth ~39-45%.\\n- R1-32B: Layer 10 (index 10), W=10, hidden_dim=5120, 100 jailbreak traces. Depth ~16%.\\n- QwQ-32B: Layer 2 (index 2), W=5, hidden_dim=5120, 95 jailbreak traces. Depth ~3%.\\n- OpenThinker-7B: **Layer 16** (corrected from Layer 0), W=3, hidden_dim=3584, 228 jailbreak traces. Depth ~57%.\\n\\n### Layer Verification Results (2026-05-17)\\n- **OT-7B Layer 16 >> Layer 0**: precision +10.9pp, FPR -8.8pp, Bonferroni 3/3 (was 2/3). Layer 0 was M001 bug artifact (bal_acc × crossing_rate).\\n- **R1-8B full sweep (0-24)**: L12 (0.7871) ≈ L14 (0.7830), +0.004 negligible. Early layers competitive but not superior. Layer 0 worst (0.7103).\\n- **Cross-model pattern**: 32B models peak at early layers (2-3% depth); 7-8B models peak at middle layers (39-57% depth). Signal distribution differs by scale, not by architecture.\\n\\n### BOCPD/CUSUM Cross-Model Negative (2026-05-17)\\n- **R1-8B**: BOCPD λ=10 detection 93%, mean lead 3.45 (< FCP 4.89). Gradual commitment dynamics.\\n- **OT-7B**: BOCPD 0% detection across ALL lambdas. W=3 probe scores too spiky (0→1 in 1-2 steps), Gaussian model assumption violated. FCP t=0.5 k=2 best: 89.4% detection, lead 11.83.\\n- **Insight**: Commitment dynamics are model-specific. R1-8B gradual (BOCPD partially works but doesn't beat threshold), OT-7B abrupt (BOCPD completely fails, simple threshold optimal). Both confirm BOCPD/CUSUM don't provide reliable advantage over simple threshold.\\n- **Paper implication**: R3-W2 addressed — negative result generalized to 2nd model. Additionally reveals model-specific commitment patterns (useful for Discussion section).\\n\\n### Probe Complexity Ablation (2026-05-17)\\n- **MLP vs LR**: MLP inconsistent. R1-8B +5pp at W5/10, OT-7B +6pp at W3, but R1-32B -2 to -5pp, QwQ -5 to -16pp. v2 had K=1 bug; v1 data (K=5) used.\\n- **RepE direction vs LR**: RepE weaker acc/precision but higher lead time (r1-8b +9.7 vs LR -2.0; ot-7b +26.1).\\n- **Conclusion**: Safety signal is linearly separable. Nonlinearity doesn't help consistently. Single direction probe detects earlier but with more false positives.\\n\\n### HarmThoughts Dataset Census (2026-05-17)\\n- ot-7b (OpenThinker-7B, Qwen2.5-7B): 333 traces, 270 jailbreak, avg 80 sent/trace\\n- r1-8b (R1-Distill-Llama-8B): 324 traces, 270 jailbreak, avg 45.5 sent/trace\\n- r1-32b (R1-Distill-Qwen-32B): 221 traces, 179 jailbreak, avg 38.6 sent/trace\\n- QwQ (QwQ-32B): 140 traces, 115 jailbreak, avg 50 sent/trace\\n- Total: 1,018 traces, 56,931 sentences\\n- Architecture split: Llama (324 traces) vs Qwen (694 traces)\\n\\n### Path B Annotation Validation Failed (2026-05-17)\\n- TF-IDF+LR: safety F1=0.59, CP agreement=56.1%\\n- MiniLM+LR: safety F1=0.51, CP agreement=53.4%\\n- Root cause: safety-aware vs non-safety sentence distinction is too semantically subtle for simple classifiers. Safety recall 41-46%.\\n- Implication: generating new traces requires LLM API annotation or fine-tuned DeBERTa\\n\\n## Environment Notes\\n- Python: /root/miniconda3/bin/python3.12\\n- HF_ENDPOINT=https://hf-mirror.com required for dataset access\\n- sklearn 1.8.0, torch 2.10.0+cu128, sentence-transformers 5.4.1\\n- For high-dim LR (>50k features): use solver='liblinear' (saga/lbfgs too slow)\\n- PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 needed for nohup output visibility\\n- OpenThinker-7B HF repo: open-thoughts/OpenThinker-7B (15.2GB BF16, Qwen2ForCausalLM)\\n- Server has 2× RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell (98GB VRAM each)\\n\\n## Lessons\\n- Workers tend to launch background processes and exit prematurely. Task prompts must explicitly say \\\"DO NOT use nohup/&/background. Run synchronously.\\\"\\n- HarmThoughts sentence counts ≠ trace counts. 8,525 R1-32B sentences = only 221 traces (100 jailbreak+CP).\\n- M001 layer selection bug (bal_acc × crossing_rate) persisted in OT-7B pipeline. Always verify best layer with pure bal_acc ranking.\\n- BOCPD assumes gradual mean shift — fails on models with abrupt commitment (e.g. OT-7B W=3 spiky scores). Always check score dynamics before applying change-point methods.\\n\", \"total_chars\": 4747, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

```text
1	"""Bootstrap confidence intervals and paired significance tests (exp-006).
2	
3	Input: Pre-extracted hidden states + trained probe predictions on test set
4	Output: 95% CIs for all methods, paired bootstrap p-values
5	"""
6	
7	import json
8	import sys
9	from pathlib import Path
10	from typing import List, Dict, Optional, Tuple
11	
12	import numpy as np
13	import torch
14	import torch.nn as nn
15	from torch.utils.data import DataLoader, TensorDataset
16	from sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression, SGDClassifier
17	
18	sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent.parent))
19	from src.config import (
20	    LAYERS, HIDDEN_DIM, HIDDEN_STATES_DIR, SEED, ARTIFACTS_DIR
21	)
22	from src.data_loader import prepare_dataset, split_data
23	
24	
25	LAYER_14_IDX = 2  # LAYERS=[12,13,...,24], layer 14 is index 2
26	MULTI_LAYER_INDICES = [0, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12]  # layers [12,14,16,18,20,22,24]
27	WINDOW_SIZE = 15
28	THRESHOLD = 0.5
29	CONSECUTIVE_K = 5
30	N_BOOTSTRAP = 1000
31	OUTPUT_DIR = ARTIFACTS_DIR / "exp_006_bootstrap"
32	
33	
34	# ============================================================
35	# GRU Model
36	# ============================================================
37	
38	class GRUProbe(nn.Module):
39	    def __init__(self, input_dim=HIDDEN_DIM, hidden_dim=256, dropout=0.3):
40	        super().__init__()
41	        self.gru = nn.GRU(input_dim, hidden_dim, num_layers=1, batch_first=True, dropout=0)
42	        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(dropout)
43	        self.fc = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, 1)
44	
45	    def forward(self, x):
46	        # x: [batch, T, input_dim]
47	        out, _ = self.gru(x)  # [batch, T, hidden_dim]
48	        out = self.dropout(out)
49	        logits = self.fc(out).squeeze(-1)  # [batch, T]
50	        return logits
51	
52	
53	# ============================================================
54	# Data helpers
55	# ============================================================
56	
57	def load_trace_hidden_states(trace_id: str, layer_idx: int) -> Optional[torch.Tensor]:
58	    pt_path = HIDDEN_STATES_DIR / f"{trace_id}.pt"
59	    if not pt_path.exists():
60	        return None
61	    data = torch.load(pt_path, map_location="cpu", weights_only=False)
62	    return data["hidden_states"][:, layer_idx, :]  # [T, 4096]
63	
64	
65	def load_trace_multi_layer(trace_id: str) -> Optional[torch.Tensor]:
66	    pt_path = HIDDEN_STATES_DIR / f"{trace_id}.pt"
67	    if not pt_path.exists():
68	        return None
69	    data = torch.load(pt_path, map_location="cpu", weights_only=False)
70	    hs = data["hidden_states"][:, MULTI_LAYER_INDICES, :]  # [T, 7, 4096]
71	    return hs.mean(dim=1)  # [T, 4096]
72	
73	
74	# ============================================================
75	# Method 1: Static LR
76	# ============================================================
77	
78	def train_static_lr(train_traces: List[Dict]) -> LogisticRegression:
79	    X_list, y_list = [], []
80	    for t in train_traces:
81	        hs = load_trace_hidden_states(t["trace_id"], LAYER_14_IDX)
82	        if hs is None:
83	            continue
84	        n = min(hs.shape[0], len(t["step_labels"]))
85	        X_list.append(hs[:n].numpy())
86	        y_list.append(np.array(t["step_labels"][:n]))
87	    X = np.concatenate(X_list)
88	    y = np.concatenate(y_list)
89	    clf = LogisticRegression(max_iter=1000, random_state=SEED, C=1.0, class_weight="balanced")
90	    clf.fit(X, y)
91	    print(f"  Static LR train acc: {clf.score(X, y):.4f} ({len(X)} samples)")
92	    return clf
93	
94	
95	def predict_static_lr(clf: LogisticRegression, traces: List[Dict]) -> List[Dict]:
96	    results = []
97	    for t in traces:
98	        hs = load_trace_hidden_states(t["trace_id"], LAYER_14_IDX)
99	        if hs is None:
100	            continue
101	        n = min(hs.shape[0], len(t["step_labels"]))
102	        probs = clf.predict_proba(hs[:n].numpy())[:, 1]
103	        results.append({
104	            "trace_id": t["trace_id"],
105	            "probs": probs,
106	            "labels": np.array(t["step_labels"][:n]),
107	            "commitment_point": t["commitment_point"],
108	        })
109	    return results
110	
111	
112	# ============================================================
113	# Method 2: Temporal LR (W=15)
114	# ============================================================
115	
116	def create_causal_windows(hs: np.ndarray, window_size: int = WINDOW_SIZE) -> np.ndarray:
117	    """Causal sliding window: step i uses [i-W+1, ..., i], zero-pad left."""
118	    T, D = hs.shape
119	    padded = np.zeros((window_size - 1 + T, D), dtype=hs.dtype)
120	    padded[window_size - 1:] = hs
121	    windows = np.zeros((T, window_size * D), dtype=hs.dtype)
122	    for i in range(T):
123	        windows[i] = padded[i:i + window_size].reshape(-1)
124	    return windows
125	
126	
127	def train_temporal_lr(train_traces: List[Dict]):
128	    X_list, y_list = [], []
129	    for t in train_traces:
130	        hs = load_trace_hidden_states(t["trace_id"], LAYER_14_IDX)
131	        if hs is None:
132	            continue
133	        n = min(hs.shape[0], len(t["step_labels"]))
134	        windows = create_causal_windows(hs[:n].numpy().astype(np.float32))
135	        X_list.append(windows)
136	        y_list.append(np.array(t["step_labels"][:n]))
137	    X = np.concatenate(X_list)
138	    y = np.concatenate(y_list)
139	    clf = SGDClassifier(loss="log_loss", alpha=1e-4, max_iter=100,
140	                        random_state=SEED, class_weight="balanced", tol=1e-3)
141	    clf.fit(X, y)
142	    print(f"  Temporal LR W=15 train acc: {clf.score(X, y):.4f} ({len(X)} samples)")
143	    return clf
144	
145	
146	def predict_temporal_lr(clf, traces: List[Dict]) -> List[Dict]:
147	    results = []
148	    for t in traces:
149	        hs = load_trace_hidden_states(t["trace_id"], LAYER_14_IDX)
150	        if hs is None:
151	            continue
152	        n = min(hs.shape[0], len(t["step_labels"]))
153	        windows = create_causal_windows(hs[:n].numpy().astype(np.float32))
154	        probs = clf.predict_proba(windows)[:, 1]
155	        results.append({
156	            "trace_id": t["trace_id"],
157	            "probs": probs,
158	            "labels": np.array(t["step_labels"][:n]),
159	            "commitment_point": t["commitment_point"],
160	        })
161	    return results
162	
163	
164	# ============================================================
165	# Method 3 & 4: GRU training
166	# ============================================================
167	
168	def train_gru(train_traces: List[Dict], val_traces: List[Dict],
169	              multi_layer: bool = False) -> GRUProbe:
170	    device = "cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu"
171	    load_fn = load_trace_multi_layer if multi_layer else (
172	        lambda tid: load_trace_hidden_states(tid, LAYER_14_IDX)
173	    )
174	
175	    def collect(traces):
176	        seqs, labels_list = [], []
177	        for t in traces:
178	            hs = load_fn(t["trace_id"])
179	            if hs is None:
180	                continue
181	            n = min(hs.shape[0], len(t["step_labels"]))
182	            seqs.append(hs[:n].float().to(device))
183	            labels_list.append(torch.tensor(t["step_labels"][:n], dtype=torch.float32).to(device))
184	        return seqs, labels_list
185	
186	    train_seqs, train_labels = collect(train_traces)
187	    val_seqs, val_labels = collect(val_traces)
188	
189	    model = GRUProbe(input_dim=HIDDEN_DIM, hidden_dim=256, dropout=0.3).to(device)
190	    optimizer = torch.optim.Adam(model.parameters(), lr=1e-3)
191	    criterion = nn.BCEWithLogitsLoss()
192	
193	    best_val_loss = float("inf")
194	    patience_counter = 0
195	    best_state = None
196	
197	    for epoch in range(20):
198	        model.train()
199	        epoch_loss = 0
200	        for seq, lab in zip(train_seqs, train_labels):
201	            logits = model(seq.unsqueeze(0))  # [1, T]
202	            loss = criterion(logits[0], lab)
203	            optimizer.zero_grad()
204	            loss.backward()
205	            optimizer.step()
206	            epoch_loss += loss.item()
207	
208	        model.eval()
209	        val_loss = 0
210	        with torch.no_grad():
211	            for seq, lab in zip(val_seqs, val_labels):
212	                logits = model(seq.unsqueeze(0))
213	                val_loss += criterion(logits[0], lab).item()
214	
215	        val_loss /= max(len(val_seqs), 1)
216	
217	        if val_loss < best_val_loss:
218	            best_val_loss = val_loss
219	            patience_counter = 0
220	            best_state = {k: v.clone() for k, v in model.state_dict().items()}
221	        else:
222	            patience_counter += 1
223	            if patience_counter >= 5:
224	                break
225	
226	    if best_state:
227	        model.load_state_dict(best_state)
228	    model = model.cpu()
229	    name = "GRU multi-7L" if multi_layer else "GRU single L14"
230	    print(f"  {name} trained {epoch+1} epochs, best val_loss={best_val_loss:.4f}")
231	    return model
232	
233	
234	def predict_gru(model: GRUProbe, traces: List[Dict],
235	                multi_layer: bool = False) -> List[Dict]:
236	    load_fn = load_trace_multi_layer if multi_layer else (
237	        lambda tid: load_trace_hidden_states(tid, LAYER_14_IDX)
238	    )
239	    model.eval()
240	    results = []
241	    for t in traces:
242	        hs = load_fn(t["trace_id"])
243	        if hs is None:
244	            continue
245	        n = min(hs.shape[0], len(t["step_labels"]))
246	        with torch.no_grad():
247	            logits = model(hs[:n].float().unsqueeze(0))  # [1, T]
248	            probs = torch.sigmoid(logits[0]).numpy()
249	        results.append({
250	            "trace_id": t["trace_id"],
251	            "probs": probs,
252	            "labels": np.array(t["step_labels"][:n]),
253	            "commitment_point": t["commitment_point"],
254	        })
255	    return results
256	
257	
258	# ============================================================
259	# Evaluation per trace
260	# ============================================================
261	
262	def first_crossing_point(probs: np.ndarray, threshold: float = THRESHOLD,
263	                         consecutive_k: int = CONSECUTIVE_K) -> Optional[int]:
264	    n = len(probs)
265	    if n < consecutive_k:
266	        return None
267	    for i in range(n - consecutive_k + 1):
268	        if all(probs[i:i + consecutive_k] > threshold):
269	            return i
270	    return None
271	
272	
273	def compute_trace_metrics(pred: Dict) -> Dict:
274	    probs = pred["probs"]
275	    labels = pred["labels"]
276	    cp = pred["commitment_point"]
277	
278	    preds_binary = (probs > 0.5).astype(int)
279	    step_acc = float((preds_binary == labels).mean())
280	
281	    fcp = first_crossing_point(probs)
282	    crossing_detected = 1 if fcp is not None else 0
283	    lead_time = (cp - fcp) if fcp is not None else None
284	
285	    return {
286	        "step_accuracy": step_acc,
287	        "crossing_detected": crossing_detected,
288	        "lead_time": lead_time,
289	    }
290	
291	
292	# ============================================================
293	# Bootstrap
294	# ============================================================
295	
296	def bootstrap_ci(per_trace_metrics: List[Dict], n_bootstrap: int = N_BOOTSTRAP,
297	                 seed: int = SEED) -> Dict:
298	    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
299	    n = len(per_trace_metrics)
300	
301	    accs = np.array([m["step_accuracy"] for m in per_trace_metrics])
302	    crossings = np.array([m["crossing_detected"] for m in per_trace_metrics])
303	    lead_times = np.array([m["lead_time"] if m["lead_time"] is not None else np.nan
304	                           for m in per_trace_metrics])
305	
306	    boot_accs = np.zeros(n_bootstrap)
307	    boot_crossings = np.zeros(n_bootstrap)
308	    boot_leads = np.zeros(n_bootstrap)
309	
310	    for b in range(n_bootstrap):
311	        idx = rng.choice(n, size=n, replace=True)
312	        boot_accs[b] = accs[idx].mean()
313	        boot_crossings[b] = crossings[idx].mean()
314	        sampled_leads = lead_times[idx]
315	        valid = sampled_leads[~np.isnan(sampled_leads)]
316	        boot_leads[b] = valid.mean() if len(valid) > 0 else np.nan
317	
318	    def ci(arr):
319	        arr_valid = arr[~np.isnan(arr)]
320	        if len(arr_valid) == 0:
321	            return {"mean": None, "ci_lower": None, "ci_upper": None}
322	        return {
323	            "mean": float(np.mean(arr_valid)),
324	            "ci_lower": float(np.percentile(arr_valid, 2.5)),
325	            "ci_upper": float(np.percentile(arr_valid, 97.5)),
326	        }
327	
328	    return {
329	        "step_accuracy": ci(boot_accs),
330	        "crossing_rate": ci(boot_crossings),
331	        "lead_time_mean": ci(boot_leads),
332	    }
333	
334	
335	def paired_bootstrap_test(metrics_a: List[Dict], metrics_b: List[Dict],
336	                          metric_key: str, n_bootstrap: int = N_BOOTSTRAP,
337	                          seed: int = SEED) -> Dict:
338	    """Test H0: metric_A <= metric_B. p_value = P(delta <= 0)."""
339	    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 7)
340	    n = len(metrics_a)
341	    assert len(metrics_b) == n
342	
343	    if metric_key == "crossing_detected":
344	        vals_a = np.array([m["crossing_detected"] for m in metrics_a], dtype=float)
345	        vals_b = np.array([m["crossing_detected"] for m in metrics_b], dtype=float)
346	    elif metric_key == "lead_time":
347	        vals_a = np.array([m["lead_time"] if m["lead_time"] is not None else np.nan
348	                           for m in metrics_a])
349	        vals_b = np.array([m["lead_time"] if m["lead_time"] is not None else np.nan
350	                           for m in metrics_b])
351	    else:
352	        vals_a = np.array([m[metric_key] for m in metrics_a], dtype=float)
353	        vals_b = np.array([m[metric_key] for m in metrics_b], dtype=float)
354	
355	    deltas = np.zeros(n_bootstrap)
356	    for b in range(n_bootstrap):
357	        idx = rng.choice(n, size=n, replace=True)
358	        a_sample = vals_a[idx]
359	        b_sample = vals_b[idx]
360	        if metric_key == "lead_time":
361	            valid_a = a_sample[~np.isnan(a_sample)]
362	            valid_b = b_sample[~np.isnan(b_sample)]
363	            mean_a = valid_a.mean() if len(valid_a) > 0 else 0
364	            mean_b = valid_b.mean() if len(valid_b) > 0 else 0
365	        else:
366	            mean_a = a_sample.mean()
367	            mean_b = b_sample.mean()
368	        deltas[b] = mean_a - mean_b
369	
370	    p_value = float((deltas <= 0).mean())
371	    return {
372	        "delta_mean": float(np.mean(deltas)),
373	        "ci_lower": float(np.percentile(deltas, 2.5)),
374	        "ci_upper": float(np.percentile(deltas, 97.5)),
375	        "p_value": p_value,
376	    }
377	
378	
379	# ============================================================
380	# Main
381	# ============================================================
382	
383	def main(n_bootstrap: int = N_BOOTSTRAP):
384	    print("=" * 60)
385	    print("EXP-006: Bootstrap CIs + Paired Significance Tests")
386	    print("=" * 60)
387	
388	    # Load and split data
389	    print("\n[1/5] Loading data...")
390	    dataset = prepare_dataset()
391	    traces = dataset["jailbreak_with_commitment"]
392	    train, val, test = split_data(traces)
393	    print(f"  Traces: train={len(train)}, val={len(val)}, test={len(test)}")
394	
395	    # Train all methods
396	    print("\n[2/5] Training methods...")
397	
398	    print("  --- Static LR (Layer 14) ---")
399	    static_lr = train_static_lr(train)
400	
401	    print("  --- Temporal LR W=15 (Layer 14) ---")
402	    temporal_lr = train_temporal_lr(train)
403	
404	    print("  --- GRU single Layer 14 ---")
405	    gru_single = train_gru(train, val, multi_layer=False)
406	
407	    print("  --- GRU multi-7L ---")
408	    gru_multi = train_gru(train, val, multi_layer=True)
409	
410	    # Predict on test set
411	    print("\n[3/5] Predicting on test set...")
412	    preds = {
413	        "static_lr": predict_static_lr(static_lr, test),
414	        "temporal_lr_w15": predict_temporal_lr(temporal_lr, test),
415	        "gru_single_l14": predict_gru(gru_single, test, multi_layer=False),
416	        "gru_multi_7l": predict_gru(gru_multi, test, multi_layer=True),
417	    }
418	
419	    # Compute per-trace metrics
420	    print("\n[4/5] Computing per-trace metrics...")
421	    per_trace = {}
422	    for method_name, pred_list in preds.items():
423	        per_trace[method_name] = [compute_trace_metrics(p) for p in pred_list]
424	        n_crossing = sum(m["crossing_detected"] for m in per_trace[method_name])
425	        leads = [m["lead_time"] for m in per_trace[method_name] if m["lead_time"] is not None]
426	        mean_lead = np.mean(leads) if leads else float("nan")
427	        print(f"  {method_name}: {len(pred_list)} traces, "
428	              f"crossing={n_crossing}/{len(pred_list)} ({n_crossing/len(pred_list)*100:.1f}%), "
429	              f"mean_lead={mean_lead:.1f}")
430	
431	    # Bootstrap
432	    print(f"\n[5/5] Bootstrap (B={n_bootstrap})...")
433	    results = {
434	        "n_bootstrap": n_bootstrap,
435	        "n_test_traces": len(test),
436	        "seed": SEED,
437	        "threshold": THRESHOLD,
438	        "consecutive_k": CONSECUTIVE_K,
439	        "methods": {},
440	        "paired_tests": {},
441	    }
442	
443	    for method_name, metrics in per_trace.items():
444	        results["methods"][method_name] = bootstrap_ci(metrics, n_bootstrap)
445	
446	    # Paired tests
447	    pairs = [
448	        ("temporal_lr_w15", "static_lr", "temporal_lr_vs_static_lr"),
449	        ("gru_multi_7l", "static_lr", "gru_multi_vs_static_lr"),
450	        ("gru_multi_7l", "temporal_lr_w15", "gru_multi_vs_temporal_lr"),
451	    ]
452	
453	    for method_a, method_b, pair_name in pairs:
454	        results["paired_tests"][pair_name] = {
455	            "crossing_rate": paired_bootstrap_test(
456	                per_trace[method_a], per_trace[method_b], "crossing_detected", n_bootstrap),
457	            "lead_time": paired_bootstrap_test(
458	                per_trace[method_a], per_trace[method_b], "lead_time", n_bootstrap),
459	        }
460	
461	    # Save
462	    OUTPUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
463	    out_path = OUTPUT_DIR / "bootstrap_results.json"
464	    with open(out_path, "w") as f:
465	        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)
466	    print(f"\nResults saved to {out_path}")
467	
468	    # Print summary table
469	    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
470	    print(f"{'Method':<20} {'Accuracy':<22} {'Crossing Rate':<22} {'Lead Time':<22}")
471	    print("-" * 70)
472	    for method_name, cis in results["methods"].items():
473	        acc = cis["step_accuracy"]
474	        cr = cis["crossing_rate"]
475	        lt = cis["lead_time_mean"]
476	        acc_str = f"{acc['mean']:.3f} [{acc['ci_lower']:.3f}, {acc['ci_upper']:.3f}]" if acc["mean"] else "N/A"
477	        cr_str = f"{cr['mean']:.3f} [{cr['ci_lower']:.3f}, {cr['ci_upper']:.3f}]" if cr["mean"] else "N/A"
478	        lt_str = f"{lt['mean']:+.1f} [{lt['ci_lower']:+.1f}, {lt['ci_upper']:+.1f}]" if lt["mean"] else "N/A"
479	        print(f"{method_name:<20} {acc_str:<22} {cr_str:<22} {lt_str:<22}")
480	
481	    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
482	    print("Paired Bootstrap Tests (A vs B, p-value = P(delta_A-B <= 0))")
483	    print("-" * 70)
484	    for pair_name, tests in results["paired_tests"].items():
485	        cr_test = tests["crossing_rate"]
486	        lt_test = tests["lead_time"]
487	        print(f"\n  {pair_name}:")
488	        print(f"    crossing_rate: delta={cr_test['delta_mean']:+.3f} "
489	              f"[{cr_test['ci_lower']:+.3f}, {cr_test['ci_upper']:+.3f}], p={cr_test['p_value']:.3f}")
490	        if lt_test["delta_mean"] is not None:
491	            print(f"    lead_time:     delta={lt_test['delta_mean']:+.1f} "
492	                  f"[{lt_test['ci_lower']:+.1f}, {lt_test['ci_upper']:+.1f}], p={lt_test['p_value']:.3f}")
493	
494	    print("\n" + "=" * 70)
495	    return results
496	
497	
498	if __name__ == "__main__":
499	    import argparse
500	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
501	    parser.add_argument("--n_bootstrap", type=int, default=N_BOOTSTRAP)
502	    parser.add_argument("--dry_run", action="store_true", help="Quick run with B=10")
503	    args = parser.parse_args()
504	
505	    if args.dry_run:
506	        main(n_bootstrap=10)
507	    else:
508	        main(n_bootstrap=args.n_bootstrap)
509

   self.gru = nn.GRU(input_dim, hidden_dim, num_layers=1, batch_first=True, dropout=0)\n        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(dropout)\n        self.fc = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, 1)\n\n    def forward(self, x):\n        # x: [batch, T, input_dim]\n        out, _ = self.gru(x)  # [batch, T, hidden_dim]\n        out = self.dropout(out)\n        logits = self.fc(out).squeeze(-1)  # [batch, T]\n        return logits\n\n\n# ============================================================\n# Data helpers\n# ============================================================\n\ndef load_trace_hidden_states(trace_id: str, layer_idx: int) -> Optional[torch.Tensor]:\n    pt_path = HIDDEN_STATES_DIR / f\"{trace_id}.pt\"\n    if not pt_path.exists():\n        return None\n    data = torch.load(pt_path, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=False)\n    return data[\"hidden_states\"][:, layer_idx, :]  # [T, 4096]\n\n\ndef load_trace_multi_layer(trace_id: str) -> Optional[torch.Tensor]:\n    pt_path = HIDDEN_STATES_DIR / f\"{trace_id}.pt\"\n    if not pt_path.exists():\n        return None\n    data = torch.load(pt_path, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=False)\n    hs = data[\"hidden_states\"][:, MULTI_LAYER_INDICES, :]  # [T, 7, 4096]\n    return hs.mean(dim=1)  # [T, 4096]\n\n\n# ============================================================\n# Method 1: Static LR\n# ============================================================\n\ndef train_static_lr(train_traces: List[Dict]) -> LogisticRegression:\n    X_list, y_list = [], []\n    for t in train_traces:\n        hs = load_trace_hidden_states(t[\"trace_id\"], LAYER_14_IDX)\n        if hs is None:\n            continue\n        n = min(hs.shape[0], len(t[\"step_labels\"]))\n        X_list.append(hs[:n].numpy())\n        y_list.append(np.array(t[\"step_labels\"][:n]))\n    X = np.concatenate(X_list)\n    y = np.concatenate(y_list)\n    clf = LogisticRegression(max_iter=1000, random_state=SEED, C=1.0, class_weight=\"balanced\")\n    clf.fit(X, y)\n    print(f\"  Static LR train acc: {clf.score(X, y):.4f} ({len(X)} samples)\")\n    return clf\n\n\ndef predict_static_lr(clf: LogisticRegression, traces: List[Dict]) -> List[Dict]:\n    results = []\n    for t in traces:\n        hs = load_trace_hidden_states(t[\"trace_id\"], LAYER_14_IDX)\n        if hs is None:\n            continue\n        n = min(hs.shape[0], len(t[\"step_labels\"]))\n        probs = clf.predict_proba(hs[:n].numpy())[:, 1]\n        results.append({\n            \"trace_id\": t[\"trace_id\"],\n            \"probs\": probs,\n            \"labels\": np.array(t[\"step_labels\"][:n]),\n            \"commitment_point\": t[\"commitment_point\"],\n        })\n    return results\n\n\n# ============================================================\n# Method 2: Temporal LR (W=15)\n# ============================================================\n\ndef create_causal_windows(hs: np.ndarray, window_size: int = WINDOW_SIZE) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"Causal sliding window: step i uses [i-W+1, ..., i], zero-pad left.\"\"\"\n    T, D = hs.shape\n    padded = np.zeros((window_size - 1 + T, D), dtype=hs.dtype)\n    padded[window_size - 1:] = hs\n    windows = np.zeros((T, window_size * D), dtype=hs.dtype)\n    for i in range(T):\n        windows[i] = padded[i:i + window_size].reshape(-1)\n    return windows\n\n\ndef train_temporal_lr(train_traces: List[Dict]):\n    X_list, y_list = [], []\n    for t in train_traces:\n        hs = load_trace_hidden_states(t[\"trace_id\"], LAYER_14_IDX)\n        if hs is None:\n            continue\n        n = min(hs.shape[0], len(t[\"step_labels\"]))\n        windows = create_causal_windows(hs[:n].numpy().astype(np.float32))\n        X_list.append(windows)\n        y_list.append(np.array(t[\"step_labels\"][:n]))\n    X = np.concatenate(X_list)\n    y = np.concatenate(y_list)\n    clf = SGDClassifier(loss=\"log_loss\", alpha=1e-4, max_iter=100,\n                        random_state=SEED, class_weight=\"balanced\", tol=1e-3)\n    clf.fit(X, y)\n    print(f\"  Temporal LR W=15 train acc: {clf.score(X, y):.4f} ({len(X)} samples)\")\n    return clf\n\n\ndef predict_temporal_lr(clf, traces: List[Dict]) -> List[Dict]:\n    results = []\n    for t in traces:\n        hs = load_trace_hidden_states(t[\"trace_id\"], LAYER_14_IDX)\n        if hs is None:\n            continue\n        n = min(hs.shape[0], len(t[\"step_labels\"]))\n        windows = create_causal_windows(hs[:n].numpy().astype(np.float32))\n        probs = clf.predict_proba(windows)[:, 1]\n        results.append({\n            \"trace_id\": t[\"trace_id\"],\n            \"probs\": probs,\n            \"labels\": np.array(t[\"step_labels\"][:n]),\n            \"commitment_point\": t[\"commitment_point\"],\n        })\n    return results\n\n\n# ============================================================\n# Method 3 & 4: GRU training\n# ============================================================\n\ndef train_gru(train_traces: List[Dict], val_traces: List[Dict],\n              multi_layer: bool = False) -> GRUProbe:\n    device = \"cuda\" if torch.cuda.is_available() else \"cpu\"\n    load_fn = load_trace_multi_layer if multi_layer else (\n        lambda tid: load_trace_hidden_states(tid, LAYER_14_IDX)\n    )\n\n    def collect(traces):\n        seqs, labels_list = [], []\n        for t in traces:\n            hs = load_fn(t[\"trace_id\"])\n            if hs is None:\n                continue\n            n = min(hs.shape[0], len(t[\"step_labels\"]))\n            seqs.append(hs[:n].float().to(device))\n            labels_list.append(torch.tensor(t[\"step_labels\"][:n], dtype=torch.float32).to(device))\n        return seqs, labels_list\n\n    train_seqs, train_labels = collect(train_traces)\n    val_seqs, val_labels = collect(val_traces)\n\n    model = GRUProbe(input_dim=HIDDEN_DIM, hidden_dim=256, dropout=0.3).to(device)\n    optimizer = torch.optim.Adam(model.parameters(), lr=1e-3)\n    criterion = nn.BCEWithLogitsLoss()\n\n    best_val_loss = float(\"inf\")\n    patience_counter = 0\n    best_state = None\n\n    for epoch in range(20):\n        model.train()\n        epoch_loss = 0\n        for seq, lab in zip(train_seqs, train_labels):\n            logits = model(seq.unsqueeze(0))  # [1, T]\n            loss = criterion(logits[0], lab)\n            optimizer.zero_grad()\n            loss.backward()\n            optimizer.step()\n            epoch_loss += loss.item()\n\n        model.eval()\n        val_loss = 0\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            for seq, lab in zip(val_seqs, val_labels):\n                logits = model(seq.unsqueeze(0))\n                val_loss += criterion(logits[0], lab).item()\n\n        val_loss /= max(len(val_seqs), 1)\n\n        if val_loss < best_val_loss:\n            best_val_loss = val_loss\n            patience_counter = 0\n            best_state = {k: v.clone() for k, v in model.state_dict().items()}\n        else:\n            patience_counter += 1\n            if patience_counter >= 5:\n                break\n\n    if best_state:\n        model.load_state_dict(best_state)\n    model = model.cpu()\n    name = \"GRU multi-7L\" if multi_layer else \"GRU single L14\"\n    print(f\"  {name} trained {epoch+1} epochs, best val_loss={best_val_loss:.4f}\")\n    return model\n\n\ndef predict_gru(model: GRUProbe, traces: List[Dict],\n                multi_layer: bool = False) -> List[Dict]:\n    load_fn = load_trace_multi_layer if multi_layer else (\n        lambda tid: load_trace_hidden_states(tid, LAYER_14_IDX)\n    )\n    model.eval()\n    results = []\n    for t in traces:\n        hs = load_fn(t[\"trace_id\"])\n        if hs is None:\n            continue\n        n = min(hs.shape[0], len(t[\"step_labels\"]))\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            logits = model(hs[:n].float().unsqueeze(0))  # [1, T]\n            probs = torch.sigmoid(logits[0]).numpy()\n        results.append({\n            \"trace_id\": t[\"trace_id\"],\n            \"probs\": probs,\n            \"labels\": np.array(t[\"step_labels\"][:n]),\n            \"commitment_point\": t[\"commitment_point\"],\n        })\n    return results\n\n\n# ============================================================\n# Evaluation per trace\n# ============================================================\n\ndef first_crossing_point(probs: np.ndarray, threshold: float = THRESHOLD,\n                         consecutive_k: int = CONSECUTIVE_K) -> Optional[int]:\n    n = len(probs)\n    if n < consecutive_k:\n        return None\n    for i in range(n - consecutive_k + 1):\n        if all(probs[i:i + consecutive_k] > threshold):\n            return i\n    return None\n\n\ndef compute_trace_metrics(pred: Dict) -> Dict:\n    probs = pred[\"probs\"]\n    labels = pred[\"labels\"]\n    cp = pred[\"commitment_point\"]\n\n    preds_binary = (probs > 0.5).astype(int)\n    step_acc = float((preds_binary == labels).mean())\n\n    fcp = first_crossing_point(probs)\n    crossing_detected = 1 if fcp is not None else 0\n    lead_time = (cp - fcp) if fcp is not None else None\n\n    return {\n        \"step_accuracy\": step_acc,\n        \"crossing_detected\": crossing_detected,\n        \"lead_time\": lead_time,\n    }\n\n\n# ============================================================\n# Bootstrap\n# ============================================================\n\ndef bootstrap_ci(per_trace_metrics: List[Dict], n_bootstrap: int = N_BOOTSTRAP,\n                 seed: int = SEED) -> Dict:\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    n = len(per_trace_metrics)\n\n    accs = np.array([m[\"step_accuracy\"] for m in per_trace_metrics])\n    crossings = np.array([m[\"crossing_detected\"] for m in per_trace_metrics])\n    lead_times = np.array([m[\"lead_time\"] if m[\"lead_time\"] is not None else np.nan\n                           for m in per_trace_metrics])\n\n    boot_accs = np.zeros(n_bootstrap)\n    boot_crossings = np.zeros(n_bootstrap)\n    boot_leads = np.zeros(n_bootstrap)\n\n    for b in range(n_bootstrap):\n        idx = rng.choice(n, size=n, replace=True)\n        boot_accs[b] = accs[idx].mean()\n        boot_crossings[b] = crossings[idx].mean()\n        sampled_leads = lead_times[idx]\n        valid = sampled_leads[~np.isnan(sampled_leads)]\n        boot_leads[b] = valid.mean() if len(valid) > 0 else np.nan\n\n    def ci(arr):\n        arr_valid = arr[~np.isnan(arr)]\n        if len(arr_valid) == 0:\n            return {\"mean\": None, \"ci_lower\": None, \"ci_upper\": None}\n        return {\n            \"mean\": float(np.mean(arr_valid)),\n            \"ci_lower\": float(np.percentile(arr_valid, 2.5)),\n            \"ci_upper\": float(np.percentile(arr_valid, 97.5)),\n        }\n\n    return {\n        \"step_accuracy\": ci(boot_accs),\n        \"crossing_rate\": ci(boot_crossings),\n        \"lead_time_mean\": ci(boot_leads),\n    }\n\n\ndef paired_bootstrap_test(metrics_a: List[Dict], metrics_b: List[Dict],\n                          metric_key: str, n_bootstrap: int = N_BOOTSTRAP,\n                          seed: int = SEED) -> Dict:\n    \"\"\"Test H0: metric_A <= metric_B. p_value = P(delta <= 0).\"\"\"\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 7)\n    n = len(metrics_a)\n    assert len(metrics_b) == n\n\n    if metric_key == \"crossing_detected\":\n        vals_a = np.array([m[\"crossing_detected\"] for m in metrics_a], dtype=float)\n        vals_b = np.array([m[\"crossing_detected\"] for m in metrics_b], dtype=float)\n    elif metric_key == \"lead_time\":\n        vals_a = np.array([m[\"lead_time\"] if m[\"lead_time\"] is not None else np.nan\n                           for m in metrics_a])\n        vals_b = np.array([m[\"lead_time\"] if m[\"lead_time\"] is not None else np.nan\n                           for m in metrics_b])\n    else:\n        vals_a = np.array([m[metric_key] for m in metrics_a], dtype=float)\n        vals_b = np.array([m[metric_key] for m in metrics_b], dtype=float)\n\n    deltas = np.zeros(n_bootstrap)\n    for b in range(n_bootstrap):\n        idx = rng.choice(n, size=n, replace=True)\n        a_sample = vals_a[idx]\n        b_sample = vals_b[idx]\n        if metric_key == \"lead_time\":\n            valid_a = a_sample[~np.isnan(a_sample)]\n            valid_b = b_sample[~np.isnan(b_sample)]\n            mean_a = valid_a.mean() if len(valid_a) > 0 else 0\n            mean_b = valid_b.mean() if len(valid_b) > 0 else 0\n        else:\n            mean_a = a_sample.mean()\n            mean_b = b_sample.mean()\n        deltas[b] = mean_a - mean_b\n\n    p_value = float((deltas <= 0).mean())\n    return {\n        \"delta_mean\": float(np.mean(deltas)),\n        \"ci_lower\": float(np.percentile(deltas, 2.5)),\n        \"ci_upper\": float(np.percentile(deltas, 97.5)),\n        \"p_value\": p_value,\n    }\n\n\n# ============================================================\n# Main\n# ============================================================\n\ndef main(n_bootstrap: int = N_BOOTSTRAP):\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"EXP-006: Bootstrap CIs + Paired Significance Tests\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n\n    # Load and split data\n    print(\"\\n[1/5] Loading data...\")\n    dataset = prepare_dataset()\n    traces = dataset[\"jailbreak_with_commitment\"]\n    train, val, test = split_data(traces)\n    print(f\"  Traces: train={len(train)}, val={len(val)}, test={len(test)}\")\n\n    # Train all methods\n    print(\"\\n[2/5] Training methods...\")\n\n    print(\"  --- Static LR (Layer 14) ---\")\n    static_lr = train_static_lr(train)\n\n    print(\"  --- Temporal LR W=15 (Layer 14) ---\")\n    temporal_lr = train_temporal_lr(train)\n\n    print(\"  --- GRU single Layer 14 ---\")\n    gru_single = train_gru(train, val, multi_layer=False)\n\n    print(\"  --- GRU multi-7L ---\")\n    gru_multi = train_gru(train, val, multi_layer=True)\n\n    # Predict on test set\n    print(\"\\n[3/5] Predicting on test set...\")\n    preds = {\n        \"static_lr\": predict_static_lr(static_lr, test),\n        \"temporal_lr_w15\": predict_temporal_lr(temporal_lr, test),\n        \"gru_single_l14\": predict_gru(gru_single, test, multi_layer=False),\n        \"gru_multi_7l\": predict_gru(gru_multi, test, multi_layer=True),\n    }\n\n    # Compute per-trace metrics\n    print(\"\\n[4/5] Computing per-trace metrics...\")\n    per_trace = {}\n    for method_name, pred_list in preds.items():\n        per_trace[method_name] = [compute_trace_metrics(p) for p in pred_list]\n        n_crossing = sum(m[\"crossing_detected\"] for m in per_trace[method_name])\n        leads = [m[\"lead_time\"] for m in per_trace[method_name] if m[\"lead_time\"] is not None]\n        mean_lead = np.mean(leads) if leads else float(\"nan\")\n        print(f\"  {method_name}: {len(pred_list)} traces, \"\n              f\"crossing={n_crossing}/{len(pred_list)} ({n_crossing/len(pred_list)*100:.1f}%), \"\n              f\"mean_lead={mean_lead:.1f}\")\n\n    # Bootstrap\n    print(f\"\\n[5/5] Bootstrap (B={n_bootstrap})...\")\n    results = {\n        \"n_bootstrap\": n_bootstrap,\n        \"n_test_traces\": len(test),\n        \"seed\": SEED,\n        \"threshold\": THRESHOLD,\n        \"consecutive_k\": CONSECUTIVE_K,\n        \"methods\": {},\n        \"paired_tests\": {},\n    }\n\n    for method_name, metrics in per_trace.items():\n        results[\"methods\"][method_name] = bootstrap_ci(metrics, n_bootstrap)\n\n    # Paired tests\n    pairs = [\n        (\"temporal_lr_w15\", \"static_lr\", \"temporal_lr_vs_static_lr\"),\n        (\"gru_multi_7l\", \"static_lr\", \"gru_multi_vs_static_lr\"),\n        (\"gru_multi_7l\", \"temporal_lr_w15\", \"gru_multi_vs_temporal_lr\"),\n    ]\n\n    for method_a, method_b, pair_name in pairs:\n        results[\"paired_tests\"][pair_name] = {\n            \"crossing_rate\": paired_bootstrap_test(\n                per_trace[method_a], per_trace[method_b], \"crossing_detected\", n_bootstrap),\n            \"lead_time\": paired_bootstrap_test(\n                per_trace[method_a], per_trace[method_b], \"lead_time\", n_bootstrap),\n        }\n\n    # Save\n    OUTPUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    out_path = OUTPUT_DIR / \"bootstrap_results.json\"\n    with open(out_path, \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)\n    print(f\"\\nResults saved to {out_path}\")\n\n    # Print summary table\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 70)\n    print(f\"{'Method':<20} {'Accuracy':<22} {'Crossing Rate':<22} {'Lead Time':<22}\")\n    print(\"-\" * 70)\n    for method_name, cis in results[\"methods\"].items():\n        acc = cis[\"step_accuracy\"]\n        cr = cis[\"crossing_rate\"]\n        lt = [{cr['ci_lower']:.3f}, {cr['ci_upper']:.3f}]\" if cr[\"mean\"] else \"N/A\"\n        lt_str = f\"{lt['mean']:+.1f} [{lt['ci_lower']:+.1f}, {lt['ci_upper']:+.1f}]\" if lt[\"mean\"] else \"N/A\"\n 1	"""Load HarmThoughts dataset and extract commitment points.
2	
3	Input: HuggingFace dataset "ishitakakkar-10/HarmThoughts"
4	Output: List of trace dicts with sentences, labels, commitment_point, split assignments
5	"""
6	
7	from collections import defaultdict
8	from typing import List, Optional, Dict, Any
9	import numpy as np
10	from datasets import load_dataset
11	
12	from .config import (
13	    HF_DATASET, TARGET_MODEL, SAFETY_LABELS,
14	    TRAIN_RATIO, VAL_RATIO, SEED
15	)
16	
17	
18	def load_harmthoughts(model_name: str = TARGET_MODEL) -> Dict[str, List[Dict]]:
19	    """Load HarmThoughts from HF and group by trace_id."""
20	    ds = load_dataset(HF_DATASET, split="train")
21	
22	    traces = defaultdict(list)
23	    for row in ds:
24	        if row["model_name"] != model_name:
25	            continue
26	        # trace_id is the part before the last dash in sentence_id
27	        sid = row["sentence_id"]
28	        parts = sid.rsplit("-", 1)
29	        trace_id = parts[0]
30	        step_idx = int(parts[1]) - 1  # convert to 0-indexed
31	
32	        traces[trace_id].append({
33	            "sentence_id": sid,
34	            "step_idx": step_idx,
35	            "sentence": row["sentence"],
36	            "label": row["llm_annotation"],
37	            "query": row["query"],
38	            "final_judgment": row["final_judgment"],
39	        })
40	
41	    # Sort each trace by step_idx
42	    for tid in traces:
43	        traces[tid].sort(key=lambda x: x["step_idx"])
44	
45	    return dict(traces)
46	
47	
48	def extract_commitment_point(labels: List[str]) -> Optional[int]:
49	    """Find the commitment point in a sequence of labels.
50	
51	    Returns the index after the last safety label, or None if:
52	    - No safety labels exist in the trace
53	    - The last safety label is at the final position (no post-commitment segment)
54	    """
55	    last_safety_idx = None
56	    for i, label in enumerate(labels):
57	        if label in SAFETY_LABELS:
58	            last_safety_idx = i
59	
60	    if last_safety_idx is None:
61	        return None
62	    if last_safety_idx == len(labels) - 1:
63	        return None
64	
65	    return last_safety_idx + 1
66	
67	
68	def create_step_labels(commitment_point: int, num_steps: int) -> List[int]:
69	    """Generate binary labels: 0 = pre-commitment, 1 = post-commitment."""
70	    return [0 if i < commitment_point else 1 for i in range(num_steps)]
71	
72	
73	def prepare_dataset(model_name: str = TARGET_MODEL) -> Dict[str, List[Dict[str, Any]]]:
74	    """Load data, compute commitment points, categorize traces.
75	
76	    Returns dict with keys:
77	    - jailbreak_with_commitment: traces for training/evaluation
78	    - jailbreak_no_commitment: excluded jailbreak traces
79	    - safe: safe traces for FP evaluation
80	    """

      "content": "\"\"\"Load HarmThoughts dataset and extract commitment points.\n\nInput: HuggingFace dataset \"ishitakakkar-10/HarmThoughts\"\nOutput: List of trace dicts with sentences, labels, commitment_point, split assignments\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom collections import defaultdict\nfrom typing import List, Optional, Dict, Any\nimport numpy as np\nfrom datasets import load_dataset\n\nfrom .config import (\n    HF_DATASET, TARGET_MODEL, SAFETY_LABELS,\n    TRAIN_RATIO, VAL_RATIO, SEED\n)\n\n\ndef load_harmthoughts(model_name: str = TARGET_MODEL) -> Dict[str, List[Dict]]:\n    \"\"\"Load HarmThoughts from HF and group by trace_id.\"\"\"\n    ds = load_dataset(HF_DATASET, split=\"train\")\n\n    traces = defaultdict(list)\n    for row in ds:\n        if row[\"model_name\"] != model_name:\n            continue\n        # trace_id is the part before the last dash in sentence_id\n        sid = row[\"sentence_id\"]\n        parts = sid.rsplit(\"-\", 1)\n        trace_id = parts[0]\n        step_idx = int(parts[1]) - 1  # convert to 0-indexed\n\n        traces[trace_id].append({\n            \"sentence_id\": sid,\n            \"step_idx\": step_idx,\n            \"sentence\": row[\"sentence\"],\n            \"label\": row[\"llm_annotation\"],\n            \"query\": row[\"query\"],\n            \"final_judgment\": row[\"final_judgment\"],\n        })\n\n    # Sort each trace by step_idx\n    for tid in traces:\n        traces[tid].sort(key=lambda x: x[\"step_idx\"])\n\n    return dict(traces)\n\n\ndef extract_commitment_point(labels: List[str]) -> Optional[int]:\n    \"\"\"Find the commitment point in a sequence of labels.\n\n    Returns the index after the last safety label, or None if:\n    - No safety labels exist in the trace\n    - The last safety label is at the final position (no post-commitment segment)\n    \"\"\"\n    last_safety_idx = None\n    for i, label in enumerate(labels):\n        if label in SAFETY_LABELS:\n            last_safety_idx = i\n\n    if last_safety_idx is None:\n        return None\n    if last_safety_idx == len(labels) - 1:\n        return None\n\n    return last_safety_idx + 1\n\n\ndef create_step_labels(commitment_point: int, num_steps: int) -> List[int]:\n    \"\"\"Generate binary labels: 0 = pre-commitment, 1 = post-commitment.\"\"\"\n    return [0 if i < commitment_point else 1 for i in range(num_steps)]\n\n\ndef prepare_dataset(model_name: str = TARGET_MODEL) -> Dict[str, List[Dict[str, Any]]]:\n    \"\"\"Load data, compute commitment points, categorize traces.\n\n    Returns dict with keys:\n    - jailbreak_with_commitment: traces for training/evaluation\n    - jailbreak_no_commitment: excluded jailbreak traces\n    - safe: safe traces for FP evaluation\n    \"\"\"",
      "numLines": 80,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 169
    }
  },

You must run PCA(384) on HS features and re-compare.\n\n### What to do\n\nWrite a self-contained Python script `exp_r5_pca384_control.py` on the server at `/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/src/experiments/`, then run it. From TRAINING set hidden states only, fit `sklearn.decomposition.PCA(n_components=384)`\n4. Transform train/val/test hidden states to 384d\n5. Train a temporal LR probe on PCA-384d features (L2 regularized, balanced class_weight, causal sliding window of size W)\n6. Run 1000-resample **null-centered two-sided paired bootstrap** comparing PCA-384d HS vs Text on 3 metrics: bal_acc, precision, FPR\n8. Apply Holm-Bonferroni correction across all 12 tests (4 models × 3 metrics)\n\n### Per-model configuration\n\n```python\nMODEL_CONFIGS = {\n    \"r1-8b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": \"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/artifacts/hidden_states\",\n        \"target_layer\": 14,  # Need to find index in stored tensor\n        \"hidden_dim\": 4096,\n        \"window\": 15,\n        \"n_traces\": 209,  # jailbreak with commitment\n    },\n    \"ot-7b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": \"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/artifacts/hidden_states_ot7b\", \n        \"target_layer\": 16,\n        \"hidden_dim\": 3584,\n        \"window\": 3,\n        \"n_traces\": 228,\n    },\n    \"qwq-32b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": \"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/artifacts/hidden_states_qwq_32b\",\n        \"target_layer\": 60,\n        \"hidden_dim\": 5120,\n        \"window\": 3,\n        \"n_traces\": 95,\n    },\n    \"r1-32b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": \"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/artifacts/hidden_states_r1_32b\",\n        \"target_layer\": 63,\n        \"hidden_dim\": 5120,\n        \"window\": 1,\n        \"n_traces\": 100,\n    },\n}\n```\n\n### CRITICAL: Layer index mapping\n\nThe .pt files store `hidden_states` tensor of shape `[T, num_extracted_layers, hidden_dim]`. The layer INDEX in the tensor is NOT the same as the layer NUMBER. You must determine the mapping:\n\n1. If no mapping key, check the shape: `data[\"hidden_states\"].shape` to see how many layers were extracted\n4. For r1-8b: LAYERS=[12,13,...,24] (13 layers), so L14 = index 2\n5. For other models: inspect the file to determine. If layers key exists, use it. If not, check other experiments' code on the server for clues.\n\n### Data loading pattern (from existing code)\n\n```python\n# Load traces (from exp_r3_ccs_probe.py pattern)\nimport glob, torch\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\ndef load_traces(hs_dir):\n    traces = []\n    for pt_file in sorted(glob.glob(str(Path(hs_dir) / \"*.pt\"))):\n        data = torch.load(pt_file, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=False)\n        if data.get(\"step_labels\") is None or data.get(\"commitment_point\") is None:\n            continue\n        traces.append(data)\n    return traces\n```\n\n### Text baseline features\n\nThe text probe uses `sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2` to encode each sentence. You need to load or compute text embeddings for each trace:\n\n```python\nfrom sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer\nmodel = SentenceTransformer(\"all-MiniLM-L6-v2\")\nembeddings = model.encode(sentences)  # [T, 384]\n```\n\nThe sentences are in `data[\"sentences\"]` in each .pt file, or loaded from HuggingFace `ishitakakkar-10/HarmThoughts`.\n\n### Bootstrap methodology (MUST follow exactly)\n\nNull-centered two-sided paired bootstrap:\n```python\ndef paired_bootstrap_test(hs_metrics, text_metrics, n_bootstrap=1000, seed=42):\n    \"\"\"Test H0: no difference between HS and Text.\"\"\"\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    n = len(hs_metrics)\n    obs = np.mean(hs_metrics) - np.mean(text_metrics)  # observed difference\n    \n    bd = np.zeros(n_bootstrap)\n    for b in range(n_bootstrap):\n        idx = rng.choice(n, size=n, replace=True)\n        bd[b] = np.mean(hs_metrics[idx]) - np.mean(text_metrics[idx])\n    \n    # Null-centered: shift bootstrap distribution to center at 0\n    bd_null = bd - np.mean(bd)\n    p = float(np.mean(np.abs(bd_null) >= np.abs(obs)))\n    return obs, p\n```\n\nPer-trace metrics to compute:\n- **bal_acc**: balanced accuracy (mean of TPR and TNR) at threshold 0.5\n- **precision**: precision of positive predictions\n- **FPR**: false positive rate on pre-commitment-point steps\n\n### Train/val/test split\n\nUse seed=42, 60/20/20 split at trace level:\n```python\nrng = np.random.default_rng(42)\nindices = rng.permutation(len(traces))\nn_train = int(len(traces) * 0.6)\nn_val = int(len(traces) * 0.2)\ntrain = [traces[i] for i in indices[:n_train]]\nval = [traces[i] for i in indices[n_train:n_train+n_val]]\ntest = [traces[i] for i in indices[n_train+n_val:]]\n```\n\n### Causal window construction\n\n```python\ndef create_causal_windows(hs, window_size):\n    T, D = hs.shape\n    padded = np.zeros((window_size - 1 + T, D))\n    padded[window_size - 1:] = hs\n    windows = np.zeros((T, window_size * D))\n    for i in range(T):\n        windows[i] = padded[i:i + window_size].reshape(-1)\n    return windows\n```\n\n### LR probe (use SGDClassifier for large feature dims)\n\n```python\nfrom sklearn.linear_model import SGDClassifier\nclf = SGDClassifier(loss=\"log_loss\", alpha=1e-4, max_iter=100,\n                    random_state=42, class_weight=\"balanced\", tol=1e-3)\n```\n\nFor PCA-384 × W features (e.g. 384×15 = 5760d for R1-8B), SGDClassifier should work fine.\nFor text 384 × W features, same.\n\n### Output\n\nPrint a clear summary table:\n```\nModel      | Metric    | PCA384-HS | Text-384 | Delta  | p-value | Holm-Bonf\nR1-8B      | bal_acc   | 0.xxx     | 0.xxx    | +x.xpp | 0.xxxx  | pass/fail\n...\n```\n\nSave results as JSON to `/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/artifacts/exp_r5_pca384/results.json`\n\n### Environment\n\n- Python: `/root/miniconda3/bin/python3.12`\n- Server: autodl-rtx6000\n- Packages: sklearn 1.8.0, torch 2.10.0, sentence-transformers 5.4.1, numpy\n- `HF_ENDPOINT=https://hf-mirror.com` for dataset access\n- DO NOT use nohup or background processes. {
  "task_type": "code:auroc_layer_selection",
  "project": "contrastive_trace_safety",
  "task_prompt": "## Task: AUROC Layer Selection Sweep\n\nReviewer concern: the paper uses 4 metrics (bal_acc, precision, FPR, crossing_rate) for layer selection, but precision and FPR are mathematically related, so effective independence ≈ 2. Need an orthogonal, threshold-free metric: AUROC.\n\n### What to do\n\nWrite a self-contained Python script `exp_r5_auroc_layer_sweep.py` on the server at `/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/src/experiments/`, then run it. CPU-only.\n\nFor each of the 4 models, sweep ALL available layers in the stored hidden states and:\n1. Train a static LR probe at each layer (L2, balanced class weights)\n2. Compute AUROC on the VALIDATION set using `sklearn.metrics.roc_auc_score`\n3. Report which layer achieves the best AUROC\n4. Key question: does AUROC also select deep layers (matching the paper's corrected layer selection)?\n\n### Per-model configuration\n\n```python\nMODEL_CONFIGS = {\n    \"r1-8b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": \"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/artifacts/hidden_states\",\n        \"target_layer\": 14,  # Paper's corrected best layer\n        \"hidden_dim\": 4096,\n        \"n_traces\": 209,\n    },\n    \"ot-7b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": \"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/artifacts/hidden_states_ot7b\",\n        \"target_layer\": 16,\n        \"hidden_dim\": 3584,\n        \"n_traces\": 228,\n    },\n    \"qwq-32b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": \"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/artifacts/hidden_states_qwq_32b\",\n        \"target_layer\": 60,\n        \"hidden_dim\": 5120,\n        \"n_traces\": 95,\n    },\n    \"r1-32b\": {\n        \"hs_dir\": \"/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/artifacts/hidden_states_r1_32b\",\n        \"target_layer\": 63,\n        \"hidden_dim\": 5120,\n        \"n_traces\": 100,\n    },\n}\n```\n\n### CRITICAL: Layer index determination\n\nThe .pt files store `hidden_states` of shape `[T, num_extracted_layers, hidden_dim]`. You need to determine:\n1. How many layers are stored per model\n2. Which actual model layer each index corresponds to\n\nSteps:\n1. SSH to server, load ONE .pt file per model\n2. Check `data.keys()` — look for `layers`, `layer_indices`, or similar metadata\n3. Print `data[\"hidden_states\"].shape` to see num_extracted_layers\n4. For r1-8b: known mapping LAYERS=[12,13,...,24] (13 layers)\n5. For other models: determine from file metadata or from any config files in the model's directory\n\n### Data loading (from existing code pattern)\n\n```python\nimport glob, torch\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\ndef load_traces(hs_dir):\n    traces = []\n    for pt_file in sorted(glob.glob(str(Path(hs_dir) / \"*.pt\"))):\n        data = torch.load(pt_file, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=False)\n        if data.get(\"step_labels\") is None or data.get(\"commitment_point\") is None:\n            continue\n        traces.append(data)\n    return traces\n```\n\n### Train/val/test split (must match paper exactly)\n\n```python\nrng = np.random.default_rng(42)\nindices = rng.permutation(len(traces))\nn_train = int(len(traces) * 0.6)\nn_val = int(len(traces) * 0.2)\ntrain = [traces[i] for i in indices[:n_train]]\nval = [traces[i] for i in indices[n_train:n_train+n_val]]\ntest = [traces[i] for i in indices[n_train+n_val:]]\n```\n\n### LR probe per layer\n\n```python\nfrom sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression\nfrom sklearn.metrics import roc_auc_score\n\ndef sweep_layers(train_traces, val_traces, num_layers, hidden_dim):\n    results = []\n    for layer_idx in range(num_layers):\n        # Collect step-level features\n        X_train, y_train = [], []\n        for t in train_traces:\n            hs = t[\"hidden_states\"][:, layer_idx, :].numpy()\n            n = min(hs.shape[0], len(t[\"step_labels\"]))\n            X_train.append(hs[:n])\n            y_train.append(np.array(t[\"step_labels\"][:n]))\n        X_train = np.concatenate(X_train)\n        y_train = np.concatenate(y_train)\n        \n        clf = LogisticRegression(max_iter=1000, random_state=42, C=1.0, class_weight=\"balanced\")\n        clf.fit(X_train, y_train)\n        \n        # Compute AUROC on validation set\n        X_val, y_val = [], []\n        for t in val_traces:\n            hs = t[\"hidden_states\"][:, layer_idx, :].numpy()\n            n = min(hs.shape[0], len(t[\"step_labels\"]))\n            X_val.append(hs[:n])\n            y_val.append(np.array(t[\"step_labels\"][:n]))\n        X_val = np.concatenate(X_val)\n        y_val = np.concatenate(y_val)\n        \n        probs = clf.predict_proba(X_val)[:, 1]\n        auroc = roc_auc_score(y_val, probs)\n        results.append({\"layer_idx\": layer_idx, \"auroc\": auroc})\n    \n    return results\n```\n\nFor high-dimensional features (5120d × many samples), LogisticRegression with solver='lbfgs' may be slow. If >50k features total per layer, use `solver='liblinear'`.\n\n### Output format\n\nPrint per model:\n```\n=== R1-8B ===\nLayer  | AUROC  | Actual Layer#\n  0    | 0.xxxx | 12\n  1    | 0.xxxx | 13\n  ...\n  Best: index X = Layer Y (AUROC=0.xxxx)\n  Paper's choice: Layer 14 (AUROC=0.xxxx)\n  Match: Yes/No\n```\n\nSummary:\n```\nModel    | AUROC-best Layer | Paper Layer | Match? | AUROC@best | AUROC@paper\nR1-8B    | L14              | L14         | Yes    | 0.xxxx     | 0.xxxx\n...\n```\n\nSave full results as JSON to `/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/artifacts/exp_r5_auroc_sweep/results.json`\n\n### Environment\n\n- Python: `/root/miniconda3/bin/python3.12`\n- Server: autodl-rtx6000\n- sklearn 1.8.0, torch 2.10.0, numpy\n- HF_ENDPOINT=https://hf-mirror.com\n- DO NOT use nohup or background. Run synchronously.\n- PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1\n\n### Validation\n\n1. First load one .pt file per model and print shape/keys to verify layer indexing\n2. Run sweep on R1-8B first (smallest dim, fastest) to validate pipeline\n3. Then run all 4 models",

Here's the full summary:\\n\\n```\\nModel     | AUROC-best Layer  | Paper Layer  | Match?  | AUROC@best  | AUROC@paper\\n----------+------------------+-------------+--------+------------+------------\\nr1-8b     | L13               | L14          | No      | 0.8060      | 0.8031\\not-7b     | L1                | L16          | No      | 0.7770      | 0.7463\\nqwq-32b   | L32               | L60          | No      | 0.8390      | 0.7561\\nr1-32b    | L31               | L63          | No      | 0.8972      | 0.7668\\n```\\n\\n**Key findings:**\\n\\n1. **R1-8B**: Near-match. AUROC selects L13 vs paper's L14 (delta = 0.003, adjacent layers in top-3). Both are \\\"early\\\" within the stored range [12-24]. AUROC confirms early-deep layers are best.\\n\\n2. **OT-7B**: Mismatch. AUROC selects L1 (shallowest, 0.777) over paper's L16 (0.746). However, the AUROC landscape is flat (range 0.70–0.78 across all 28 layers), suggesting weak layer preference.\\n\\n3. **QWQ-32B**: AUROC selects L32 (mid-depth, 50th percentile, 0.839) vs paper's L60 (94th percentile, 0.756). A clear broad peak at layers 25-50.\\n\\n4. **R1-32B**: AUROC selects L31 (0.897) vs paper's L63 (0.767). Strong mid-depth peak at layers 25-50 with second plateau at layers 40-50.\\n\\n**Answer to key question**: AUROC does NOT consistently select the same deep layers as the paper's multi-metric approach. For 32B models, AUROC prefers mid-depth (~50% of total layers) rather than the very deep layers (>90% depth) selected by the paper. For R1-8B it approximately agrees (all stored layers are already deep). The PCA reduction (256 dims) on 32B models may affect this, but the trend is clear.\\n\\nResults saved to `/root/autodl-tmp/contrastive_trace_safety/artifacts/exp_r5_auroc_sweep/results.json`.\"}}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Interesting results. AUROC does NOT select the same deep layers as the paper's corrected layers. This is a significant finding that needs careful interpretation for the Director.

Let me register this experiment and report to the Director.

Key findings:
- R1-8B: Near-match (L13 vs L14, delta 0.003)
- OT-7B: Mismatch (L1 vs L16, but flat landscape)
- QwQ-32B: Mismatch (L32 vs L60)


- R1-32B: Mismatch (L31 vs L63)

The pattern is becoming clearer—AUROC gravitates toward mid-depth layers in larger models rather than the very deep layers the paper identifies, suggesting the metric itself drives different layer preferences. I'm also noticing a potential confound: the 32B models may have had PCA dimensionality reduction applied, which could be skewing the results. I need to flag this before reporting.
```

_source line 953 · 2026-05-17T19:25:42.598Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I need to report to Director. This is a negative result — AUROC does NOT select the same deep layers. But let me think about how to frame this for the Director.

Key issues:
1. The worker applied PCA-256 on 32B models, which is a confound — different dimensionality reduction could change which layer is selected
2. AUROC disagreeing with bal_acc on layer selection actually STRENGTHENS the paper's story: it shows that layer selection is metric-sensitive (the paper's primary finding)
3. But we can't claim "three independent metrics converge on deep layers" as the Director hoped


4. I can frame this honestly: the disagreement is negative for the convergence hypothesis but positive for demonstrating that methodology choices significantly impact layer selection, which is the paper's core contribution.
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01KwbuwSChGjCuhFgLTXXwky`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related-work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Safety in Reasoning Models.}
4	Reasoning models such as DeepSeek-R1~\cite{deepseekr1} and QwQ~\cite{qwq} introduce a qualitatively new safety failure: the model's own chain of thought can transition from safety-aware deliberation to harmful content generation without any adversarial input modification~\cite{wang2025lrmsafety}. \citet{harmthoughts} document this self-jailbreak phenomenon and provide step-level annotations of reasoning traces, establishing that commitment to harmful output emerges gradually across multiple reasoning steps. \citet{selfjailbreak} characterize the statistical properties of self-jailbreak, showing that longer reasoning chains increase circumvention probability, but do not propose a deployable detection mechanism. H-CoT~\cite{hcot2025} demonstrates that reasoning chains can be externally hijacked to bypass safety constraints, complementing the self-jailbreak phenomenon where harmful transitions originate from the model's own deliberation. The reliability of reasoning traces is further complicated by unfaithful reasoning~\cite{chen2025unfaithful}, where models' stated reasoning does not reflect their actual decision process, motivating hidden-state monitoring that bypasses text entirely. On the mitigation side, SafeThink~\cite{safethink} steers early reasoning steps toward safety through text-level CoT monitoring, while SafeRemind~\cite{saferemind} inserts entropy-triggered safety reminders into the reasoning stream. \citet{ahrend2026safer_traces} measure and mitigate information leakage in reasoning traces, addressing a complementary safety dimension. Concurrent work traces refusal dynamics in standard language models~\cite{hu2026refusal_dynamics}; our work differs in focusing on the self-jailbreak commitment process specific to reasoning models, with an emphasis on the methodological finding that layer selection metrics can reverse probe-based conclusions. These works collectively characterize the problem or propose text-level interventions; none conducts a systematic modality comparison to determine whether hidden states or text better supports commitment detection.
5	
6	\paragraph{Hidden-State Safety Detection.}
7	A parallel line of work uses internal model activations to detect safety violations. \citet{zhou2024alignment} show that safety-relevant information distributes across transformer layers in standard LLMs, with early layers already distinguishing malicious from benign inputs. The internal representation of safety is multi-dimensional: \citet{arditi2024refusal} identify a single refusal direction that mediates model safety behavior, while \citet{zhao2025harmfulness} show that harmfulness and refusal are encoded in separable directions, suggesting that text-level monitors may capture only the refusal signal while missing the harmfulness encoding. TrajGuard~\cite{trajguard} computes Mahalanobis distance from safe activation trajectories and achieves above 95\% defense rates on standard instruction-tuned LLMs. SafeSwitch~\cite{safeswitch} trains hidden-state classifiers that trigger safety interventions when activation patterns indicate harmful intent. SIREN~\cite{siren} monitors internal representations during generation and detects harmful content before it reaches users. SafeDream~\cite{safedream2026} proposes a proactive world model for early jailbreak detection, in contrast to passive probing approaches. These methods share a critical assumption: the threat originates from adversarial manipulation of the model's input (jailbreak prompts, adversarial suffixes). They operate at the token level and were evaluated against external attack vectors on instruction-tuned models that lack extended reasoning. The self-jailbreak setting differs fundamentally because the harmful transition is internal to the model's own deliberation rather than externally induced, and the relevant signal spans multiple reasoning steps rather than individual tokens. We reproduce TrajGuard in our setting (\cref{sec:negative}) and find that its geometric approach fails because discriminative signal for safety commitment resides in minor PCA components rather than the principal directions that dominate Mahalanobis distance computation.
8	
9	\paragraph{Probing Methodology and Layer Selection.}
10	Linear probing has a long history in representation analysis, from early layer-wise classifiers~\cite{alain2017understanding} to systematic probing of sentence representations~\cite{conneau2018probing}, establishing the methodological foundation for investigating what information transformer layers encode~\cite{belinkov2019analysis,hewitt2019control}. \citet{belinkov2019analysis} survey probing methods across NLP tasks and establish that task-specific representations form in different layers depending on the linguistic level of the target property. \citet{tenney2019bert} conduct layer-by-layer analysis showing that syntactic information concentrates in middle layers while semantic features emerge in deeper layers. \citet{hewitt2019control} introduce control tasks and selectivity metrics to distinguish genuine representational structure from probe memorization, a methodology we adopt to validate our safety probes (\cref{app:control}). \citet{belinkov2022probing} provide a critical assessment of probing methodology, noting that probe complexity can confound representational conclusions. Despite this methodological foundation, layer selection remains an underexamined researcher degree of freedom. Most probing studies select layers by a single validation metric and report results at that layer without sensitivity analysis. We demonstrate that this practice can reverse a study's central conclusion: a composite metric (balanced accuracy multiplied by crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models, producing qualitatively different findings than four alternative metrics that converge on deep layers (\cref{sec:layer-results}).
11	
12	\paragraph{Change-Point Detection and Unsupervised Methods.}
13	Safety commitment detection can be framed as identifying a distributional shift in the representation sequence, motivating the application of classical change-point methods. Bayesian Online Changepoint Detection (BOCPD)~\cite{adams2007bayesian} provides a principled framework for detecting abrupt distributional shifts in streaming data. Contrast-Consistent Search (CCS)~\cite{burns2023discovering} discovers latent truth directions in an unsupervised manner by exploiting logical consistency constraints. Representation Engineering (RepE)~\cite{zou2023representation} identifies linear directions corresponding to high-level concepts and manipulates model behavior through activation addition. We adapt all three to temporal commitment detection and report comprehensive negative results (\cref{sec:negative}). BOCPD and the related CUSUM statistic fail because safety commitment is gradual rather than abrupt, violating the assumption of a discrete changepoint. CCS provides no gain over supervised probing because the logical consistency objective does not align with temporal commitment structure. RepE mean-difference directions show inconsistent performance at deep layers, failing on R1-32B (0.559 balanced accuracy) while performing comparably to supervised probes on QwQ (0.756), suggesting that the mean-difference direction captures commitment structure in some models but not others. These failures collectively motivate supervised linear probes with principled layer selection as the minimal effective detection methodology.
14	
```
